> help
Short walks, purchase lines and chats spend minutes within the hour; long acts finish the hour. Settings (standing, agency, rations) and queries take no time. Sleep keeps you in bed at least 4 hours (8 when you camp); wait to sleep on, or give any order (wake, drink, go) to get up.
Eat defaults to ONE serving; use eat hardtack 2 for two. Need effects settle once per hour; a partly spent hour uses its dominant activity.
Out on open ground with bedding, sleep makes camp where you stand; wait follows your lodging order, returning to a nearby paid or own bed when available.

hire <employer> [daily|weekly] | quit | dismiss <worker>
buy <item> [qty] | sell <item> [qty] | eat <item> [servings] [method] | cook <item> [servings] [method]
drink | fill <canteen/kettle> | empty <canteen/kettle> | fetch water | meal | sleep | wake | rest | work | wait <Nh|Nd> | wait until <HH:00|dawn|morning|noon|dusk|night|tomorrow>
Springs, creeks, rivers and fords are water: drink raw there and fill your vessels (a litre weighs a kilo: a fill stops where you could no longer walk, and empty pours a vessel out); fetch water walks to the nearest.

  Ellis Wayfarer: “Ellis Wayfarer. I hope we'll have occasion to talk.”

  Ellis Wayfarer: “How are the hens laying?”

  Amos Whitcomb: “I once set a corner stone, and the owner moved it overnight. Six inches.”
  Lucius Vance: “Six inches?”
  Amos Whitcomb: “It was the best six inches in the county. I let him keep it.”
  Michael Dunleavy: “Shall I split a bit of kindling for you?”
  Amos Whitcomb: “Please do. I've been pretending the box was full.”

> journal
1855-04-01 00:00 Sunday, the 1st of April, 1855. The Sabbath under a full moon; the evening service will want no lantern. Rain in the evening.
1855-04-01 07:00 Joel Pike insulted Nora Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern, and I was by to see it. Everyone pretended to be looking at something else.
1855-04-01 07:00 Michael Dunleavy helped Lucius Vance at Dunleavy's Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-02 00:00 Monday, the 2nd of April, 1855. The full moon. The bluffs stand out white and the dogs are restless.
1855-04-02 07:00 Jonas Hale made eyes at me. I did not know where to look, so I looked at him, which was the trouble.
1855-04-02 13:00 Martha Hale made eyes at Jonas Hale at Cedar Farm fields on Monday in the afternoon. Half the room pretended not to see.
1855-04-02 18:00 I shut a door in myself today. I cannot say which, only that the house is quieter.
1855-04-03 00:00 Tuesday, the 3rd of April, 1855. The full moon, which the Norwegians watch and the Cornish do not discuss.
1855-04-03 09:00 I went to bed parched. A person can learn a good deal about the world from the floor of an empty belly.
1855-04-03 19:00 I notice I am saying the same thing to myself over and over, in the same words. A person ought to be able to say a thing once and be done.
1855-04-03 19:00 Something set me against Martha Hale today, who had done nothing to earn it. Something else was the matter, and I will not name it.
1855-04-04 00:00 Wednesday, the 4th of April, 1855, under the waning gibbous. Snow in the morning.
1855-04-04 03:00 I have a great many reasons this week, and I do not trust any of them. I would rather have one true thing.
1855-04-04 03:00 It was the sensible thing; anyone would agree. I have asked myself and I agree.
1855-04-04 05:00 Amos Whitcomb had words with Michael Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern on Wednesday at first light; nothing came of it.
1855-04-04 09:00 I asked Gussie Pratt for the news at Pratt & Daughter, Outfitters. I came away knowing more than I went in with, which is the point of such talks.
1855-04-04 09:00 Lydia Pratt and I had our heads together at Pratt & Daughter, Outfitters on Wednesday in the forenoon. Whatever the town is saying, we said it too, and a little more.
1855-04-04 11:00 Something set me against Nora Dunleavy today, who had done nothing to earn it. Something else was the matter, and I will not name it.
1855-04-04 18:00 Amos Whitcomb drank a toast with Lucius Vance at Dunleavy's Tavern on Wednesday of an evening.
1855-04-05 00:00 Thursday, the 5th of April, 1855. The waning gibbous.
1855-04-05 03:00 Something set me against Nora Dunleavy today, who had done nothing to earn it. Something else was the matter, and I will not name it.
1855-04-05 16:00 I asked Jonas Hale for the news at Cedar Farm fields on Thursday in the afternoon. He had nothing new for me, or nothing he would part with.
1855-04-05 16:00 Martha Hale and I had our heads together at Cedar Farm fields on Thursday in the afternoon. Whatever the town is saying, we said it too, and a little more.
1855-04-06 00:00 Friday, the 6th of April, 1855, under the waning gibbous.
1855-04-07 00:00 Saturday, the 7th of April, 1855. The waning gibbous. Rain in the night.
1855-04-07 04:00 Something set me against Amos Whitcomb today, who had done nothing to earn it. Something else was the matter, and I will not name it.
1855-04-07 12:00 Something set me against Michael Dunleavy today, who had done nothing to earn it. Something else was the matter, and I will not name it.
1855-04-07 00:00 Week 1. The house took 2193 calories a head by the day, near enough, on swiss cheese, dried apples and hardtack (ship's biscuit). Set down also: nothing worth the naming gone.
1855-04-08 00:00 Sunday, the 8th of April, 1855. Sabbath, under the last quarter; work laid by. Rain in the morning.
1855-04-08 01:00 Amos Whitcomb stopped to talk at Dunleavy's Tavern on Sunday in the small hours. Old talk; I had it already, or there was none.
1855-04-08 03:00 Joel Pike talked with me at Dunleavy's Tavern on Sunday in the small hours.
1855-04-08 04:00 Something set me against Lucius Vance today, who had done nothing to earn it. Something else was the matter, and I will not name it.
1855-04-08 05:00 Michael Dunleavy talked with Lucius Vance at Dunleavy's Tavern on Sunday at first light.
No time passed (1855-04-08 06:00, Dunleavy's Tavern).
